| DARING HEIST
Contract #: 454987B
Employer: Night Owls
You’ve never heard of the Night Owls
before . And they’re not willing to tell you much
more . They asked to meet in a rundown bar on
some backwoods system and let the offer do
the talking . That they are, or were, Clan is fairly
obvious . At least one was a dead giveaway to
be an Elemental . But there are no Night Owls
registered as mercenaries, and no Clan force
with that name . And they make it clear they are
here to grab whatever they can take .
Location (Primary Terrain): Alyina, Alyina
Merchant League (Wooded-Jungle)
Type of Action: Diversionary Raid
Length of Contract: 3 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: 0% (1)
Transportation: 100% (9)
Salvage Rights: 100% (13)
Command Rights: House (8)
Bonus: A mercenary serving the entire length
of the contract will receive a choice of
’Mech from the Alyina Factory Zone 4 .
The Night Owls have a plan to
raid a former Clan Jade Falcon factory .
They know the defenses; they know the
way in and out . You are a contingency
plan . You will train with the Night Owls
till you all have the timing of the plan
well-rehearsed . The Night Owls will
provide transport for you, both to
and from the staging area and to and
from the raid target . You will launch
a diversionary raid against another
target, coordinated to draw attention
away when needed . You will follow
the plan, tie up any possible defender
reinforcements for the specified time,
then get out of there .
In return, the Night Owls have
offered you a share of the brand
new Clan ’Mechs stolen from the
factory floor . |   |   |

### Table 406

| BLACK MARKET
In systems with established government institutions,
customs inspections, and import controls, taxes and
tariffs are all part of regular intra-system commerce.
The Hinterlands are largely lacking such structures,
making the entire region a smuggler’s paradise. And
it remains a seller’s market, given widespread demand
from mercenaries, insurgents, struggling planetary
governments, and newly established secessionist or
successor regimes.
The Lyran Commonwealth has long supplied
weaponry to anti-Clan insurgents in the occupation
zones, and Operation Black Ice only accelerated that
trend throughout the Hinterlands. Black markets on
most worlds are awash in everything from concealable
weaponry to firepower heavy enough to cut an Elemental
in half. Pentaglycerine, C8, landmines, and other tools for
improvised roadside bombs, infrastructure destruction,
and remote-detonated assassination are also plentiful. It
is rumored that some of the teams may have even brought
suitcase nukes to crack particularly stubborn holdouts.
Many worlds that overthrew their Clan oppressors
claimed substantial amounts of Clan-tech gear, including
vehicles, combat armor, and small arms. Finding some
advanced systems difficult to maintain with their limited
resources, those who liberated the gear often unloaded
it on the black market in exchange for supplies of more
sustainable hardware. Buyers with the resources to
maintain Clan-tech gear may find willing vendors.
Black marketeers also have off-world sources and
can bring in gear far beyond just insurgent toolkits and
paramilitary police gear. Those with ties to the Malthus
Confederation, unaffiliated Free Guilds, or even the Sea
Foxes can acquire combat vehicles, battle armor, aerospace
fighters, and BattleMechs, though the markup will make
such acquisitions painful for buyers’ pocketbooks.
Successful mercenaries operating in the Hinterlands
will often find themselves in possession of “slightly used”
gear once possessed by somewhat less skilled or lucky
foes. If they are unable to make use of it themselves, black
markets are excellent places to either source the parts
necessary to restore them to functional status or to unload
them (albeit at a steep discount) and turn a quick profit.
—KevAN “KroNerhouNd” ortmAN,
profitinG froM tHe sufferinG of otHers—a ten step Guide,
revised ANd updAted 3152 editioN |   |

### Table 407

| LEGANES
APOSTICA RAID
Contract #: 103490B
Employer: Edward Urbanick, Duke of Leganes
Clan Jade Falcon may have taken Duke Urbanick’s world
from him, but no one ever knew the Duke to give up a fight . It
was the Duke who led the underground resistance movement
against them during the dark years, paying the greatest price
after his son was killed in one of the many Falcon purges .
When the Clan stripped the planet’s garrison, the Duke led
his demoralized soldiers in one final fight, overthrowing the
few Falcon overseers left in a single, bloody clash . With his
dukedom back in his hands, Urbanick now looks to build his
troops into an even stronger force—no matter what it takes .
Location (Primary Terrain): Apostica, Former Jade Falcon
Occupation Zone (Desert)
Type of Action: Raid
Length of Contract: 3 months
Base Pay: 80% (5)
Support: Battle / 30% (9)
Transportation: 50% (7)
Salvage Rights: 30% (6)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
Leganes stood on its own during the dark years . No
aid came from the Commonwealth, the Republic, or any
nearby worlds even when times were at their grimmest .
And now, with Leganes finally free of the Falcon yoke,
its neighbors both large and small come begging for
charity . Though unsurprising, it is…disappointing .
The Duke has but one concern: his rule, his world,
and his people . He will use every tool at his disposal for
their defense, even if it means feasting on Apostica’s
remains before the world reaches its final end . The
disaster that happened to the world is devastating,
but the secrets held in the recently discovered military
facility may prevent the same from occurring to worlds
that still live .
The DropShip the Duke will provide should prove
large enough to transport any hardware recovered from
the facility . Though Leganes has fewer resources than
some after so many long, hard years of fighting, the
Duke is willing to offer a greater degree of autonomy
if it leads to results . Results, however, are essential .
Betrayal will not be taken lightly . |   |

### Table 443

| CHAHAR GARRISON
Contract #: 105837A
Employer: The Council of Eight Gurus
Chahar, a center of the Hindu faith in the Lyran
Commonwealth, fell to the Jade Falcons surrounded by
rumors that the Clans have profaned the holy city of
Benares . While exiles continued to agitate for rebellion,
the people of Chahar preserved . But after several
Hindu protests against Khan Malvina Hazen’s Mongol
Doctrine, public displays were banned and the holy
city of Benares was devastated as religious sites were
torn to the ground . Practitioners continued to follow
their beliefs in secret, and Hinduism experienced a
massive resurgence following the collapse of the Jade
Falcon OZ and centralized Clan leadership . Chahar’s day-
to-day operations are now led by a coalition of eight
influential Gurus looking to reintegrate their planet into
the broader Inner Sphere economy once more .
Location (Primary Terrain): Chahar, Former Jade
Falcon Occupation Zone (Urban)
Type of Action: Garrison/Defense
Length of Contract: 6 months
Base Pay: 70% (4)
Support: Battle / 10% (7)
Transportation: 0% (5)
Salvage Rights: 100% (13)
Command Rights: House (6)
Prior to Clan Jade Falcon’s conquest of the
world, Chahar was renown for Dobless Information
Services—a data storage company boasting the
single largest concentration of knowledge in the
Inner Sphere . The Clan transformed the company
into a relatively benign research and development
center for its scientist caste, but under Malvina’s
reign, matters took a darker turn .
Ordered to focus on nuclear physics, Dobless
Information Services was transformed into the
Clan’s supplier of nuclear weapons—weapons that
were put to grim use . With Malvina’s Mongols now
purged from the planet and a relatively peaceful
government in power, the Eight seek to distance
itself from its bloody past .
Others are not so ready to abandon such
powerful weapons . The Eight are well aware that
Chahar’s wicked industry make it a tempting target,
and they seek defenders willing to protect Dobless
until its nuclear weapons and manufacturing
plant are safely dismantled . Though Chahar is not
financially rich, the Eight are able to offer a wealth
of information, technology, and Trellshire Industries
output to all hires—both of which may be worth
more than their weight in gold to the clever . | CHAHAR RAID
Contract #: 105837B
Employer: Trellshire Industries—Chahar Branch
Once a Lyran corporation, Trellshire Industries
had nearly all of its assets fallen to the Clans by the
end of the thirty-first century . Its leadership replaced
by key members of the lower caste and its lines
upgraded to Clan standards, Trellshire quickly became
synonymous with cutting-edge Clan hardware . The
crumbling of the Falcon Occupation Zone, however,
had led to a schism in Trellshire itself; while the
Sudeten and Twycross branches continued to back
their Clans, Trellshire-Chahar had fallen under the
sway of Arshad Kochhar, an ambitious CEO who
dreamed of transforming his planet into one of the
region’s greatest industrial hubs .
Location (Primary Terrain): Chahar, Former Jade
Falcon Occupation Zone (Urban)
Type of Action: Raid
Length of Contract: 6 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: Battle / 30% (9)
Transportation: 25% (6)
Salvage Rights: 20% (5)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
None have done more to support Chahar
than Trellshire Industries and its CEO, Arshar
Kochhar . During the Clan occupation, Kocchar
shielded countless Hindus from persecution,
using his power and influence to protect the
planet from the Mongols’ worst excesses . A natural
choice to lead the planet, Kocchar was instead
brushed aside by a collection of inexperienced,
populist Gurus who are rapidly leading the planet
toward disaster .
While Kochhar is unwilling to sponsor a
full-blown rebellion, current plans to dismantle
the Dobless Manufactory are unacceptable to
him . The loss of technology essential to Chahar’s
continued relevancy and future cannot be
allowed to happen unopposed, and Kochhar
seeks mercenaries who are willing to safely
capture essential Dobless matériel to bring them
back to the secure Trellshire complex .
You will be richly rewarded for your efforts;
though Chahar’s government may claim
jurisdiction over our industrial output, we have
stockpiled cutting-edge production to safeguard
our facilities and protect us from future economic
uncertainty . We can grant you access to these
supplies and can guarantee that they will give you
a leg up over any of your competitors . |

### Table 461

| ESTEROS
RECLAMATION
Contract #: 104691A
Employer: Esteros Planetary Assembly
Graf Leonhard Kayserling was the leader of the
Esteros Assembly when the world was captured by the
Jade Falcons in 3141 . The planetary assembly, made up
of noble houses, was allowed to survive and continue to
administer the planet but answered to the Jade Falcon
Clan . After the Clan abandoned the world, the Assembly
has been seeking to reassert their authority and restore
order to the world as a precursor step to reintegrating
Esteros into some greater power . Graf Kayserling has
used his contacts to seek mercenaries on nearby worlds,
such as Kandersteg, that would be sympathetic toward
his cause or at least his money .
Location (Primary Terrain): Esteros, Former Jade
Falcon Occupation Zone (Desert)
Type of Action: Pirate Hunt
Length of Contract: 3 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: Battle / 30% (9)
Transportation: 70% (7)
Salvage Rights: 25% (5)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
After the Jade Falcons abandoned Esteros,
central authority collapsed, with much of the world
falling into the hands of warlords . The surviving
noble families of the Esteros Assembly tried to
reassert their authority, only to find that the warlords
possessed larger and better-equipped armies than
expected . Realizing that their own meager forces
lacked either the numbers or the capabilities to take
on the warlords, the Esteros Assembly were forced
to hire mercenaries to restore order to their world .
The Esteros Assembly consists of noble families
who built their wealth and power off exploiting
the world’s considerable mineral resources that
were previously exported to feed industries across
the Lyran Commonwealth . When the world was
conquered by the Jade Falcons, the Clan’s merchant
caste cut the nobles out of the picture and instead
directly exploited those resources, using them to
supply manufacturing centers on Sudeten, Alyina,
and other worlds .
The Esteros Assembly are seeking to bring
order to the planet by eliminating the warlords
and securing the mines that fuel their economy .
However, the warlords are resisting these efforts,
and they have no desire to give up their power .
Both sides have hired mercenaries to supplement
their forces . |   | ESTEROS
DISRUPTION
Contract #: 104691B
Employer: Warlord Ruhi Panja
An Esteros native who worked as a middle manager
in a mining operation, Warlord Ruhi Panja had used her
prior position to become an administrator under the
Jade Falcon occupation . Fearing that she would be seen
as a collaborator and become a target for recrimination,
Panja and several other former administrators banded
together initially to protect themselves . As her strength
grew, she became emboldened and began capturing
nearby territories, carving out her own small empire .
However, she also is aware that the Assembly is seeking
to reassert their authority and has no desire to face
their justice .
Location (Primary Terrain): Esteros, Former Jade
Falcon Occupation Zone (Desert)
Type of Action: Raid
Length of Contract: 3 months
Base Pay: 70% (4)
Support: Straight / 20% (2)
Transportation: 0% (5)
Salvage Rights: 70% (10)
Command Rights: Independent (10)
As one of the Esteros warlords, Ruhi Panja
has no desire to reintegrate into Esteros society .
Between her being a supporter of the Jade Falcon
occupation and her subsequent actions, she has
no doubt that she would be forced to face justice,
one way or another . Furthermore, she has become
comfortable in her position and does not want to
bend the knee to any greater power; rather, she
seeks to carve out her own microstate on Esteros .
The presence of other warlords has bought her
some time, but she harbors no illusions that the
Assembly will come after her sooner or later .
Like the other warlords, her forces were
initially made up of conscript infantry and modified
IndustrialMechs . However, the arrival of traders has
helped her to expand that force to include combat
vehicles and even BattleMechs . But her forces are
relatively inexperienced and lack any ability to
project power beyond her own territories . The
mines that she controls have given her a potential
solution to her problems, allowing her to hire
mercenaries and pay them in rare ores and other
materials .
Panja does not want to rule Esteros herself,
but she simply wants to survive and maintain her
independence . To that end, she is seeking to weaken
both the Assembly and her rival warlords . |

### Table 486

| TRANSPORTATION
Getting around the Hinterlands can be tricky, as shipping and logistics
networks remain stressed and disrupted while captains and loadmasters
adapt to the region’s new threats and opportunities. Though mercenary
units with their own JumpShips and DropShips can charge a premium,
thanks to the extra flexibility such assets provide, maintaining their
vessels will likely require access to the shipyards at Arc-Royal, Butler, or
Sudeten. Units without integral transport are spared such concerns, but
they must make do with whatever is available to successfully fulfill their
contract requirements.
Employer-provided transportation remains a viable alternative.
The Alyina Mercantile League sent its own ships to Galatea to bring
mercenaries back, contracting with the mercenary logistics firm Steve’s
Stevedores to carry them back to the League en-masse. Contracts can
easily be structured to require employers to provide transportation, but
such reliance on an employer does place units at risk of being stranded
if the contract goes sour. Fortunately, commercial services can often
provide non-employer options.
In Clan-controlled space and in independent systems, the Free
Guilds offer the most reliable transportation services. They maintain
offices at most spaceports on worlds that aren’t rabidly anti-Clan and
offer passenger accommodations and cargo shipping. They retain
unhindered freedom to transit the Rasalhague Dominion and the Hell’s
Horses Occupation Zone.
The Malthus Confederation also maintains ties to its Free Guild roots
and offers transportation services. For a cut of the take, they will even
transport mercenary units to and from raids, as long as the targeted
world isn’t paying them for protection. It is rumored that they will offer
discounted service rates for raids against worlds that have not yet signed
up for a Malthus protection plan.
In systems that are more Lyran-leaning, vessels of the Lyran Free
Trader Association once more ply the space lanes. Work is underway by the
Meier-Star Agency and other commercial shipping firms to re-establish
regular routes to the Vesper Marches, Arc-Royal Defense Cordon, and
Tamar Pact. The Archon’s frosty attitude toward the Marches and the Pact
has not stopped Lyran merchants from re-entering these markets.
—the hiNterlANds,
MerCenaries’ Guild MeMber advisory, 3152 |   |

### Table 490

| RODEO RAID MISSION BRIEF
Contract #: 950014A
Intensity (Number of Tracks): 2–3
Behind the Scenes: There are two initial tracks in this contract, with a possible third, depending on the success of
the first two . The initial job is as stated, with no real trickery behind the scenes . Pettersson expects anonymity to be
preserved at all costs in the initial attack .
The real complications come in the acquisition and holding on to the hell’s horse . The animals are notoriously
wild and untamable, and Star Colonel David Fletcher is only able to keep his as a mascot by having an overly large
ranging area . That, and he’s discovered a local fauna the horse is quite fond of .
Pettersson also wants to avoid extensive casualties as a gesture of goodwill toward the Clan and the merchant
caste and to showcase his own feelings of superiority .
While an absolutely arrogant, Type A personality jerk, Pettersson will treat the unit fairly in negotiations . To a
point, anyway . He’s not about to let them roll over with requests and extra concessions, but he very much wants this
mission to succeed and is willing to “spend money to make money .”
Month 1 is spent in travel .
Month 2 begins with Track 1, Objective Raid . They must then do Track 2, Pursuit .
Month 3 completes the contract if the mercenaries successfully
completed Track 2 .
If the mercenaries do not successfully complete Track 2, they must MAPSHEET
do Track 3, Defend . TABLE
Then roll 1D6 to determine the mapsheet(s) to be played on .

### Table 505

| BLUE MARTENS COVERT
OPERATIONS
Contract #: 11001156A
Employer: Lyran Commonwealth Intelligence Corps
Emilijia Saric is a not so secret agent of the Lyran
Commonwealth Intelligence Corps . Her charm and the money
in the accounts she maintains keep mercenaries listening to her
offers even if the details are a little vague . Plenty of mercenaries
will vouch the money is good, though just as many complain
that Lyran intel is an oxymoron .
Location (Primary Terrain): Krievci, Former Clan Jade Falcon
Occupation Zone (Desert)
Type of Action: Covert Operations
Length of Contract: 6 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: 0% (1)
Transportation: 0% (5)
Salvage Rights: 100% (13)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
The Lyran Commonwealth, or at least its Archon, wants
to avoid war . But opportunities exist that may not require
one . The Lyran Intelligence Corps believes Krievci is one such
opportunity . The system has never had a heavy Clan garrison,
and the Krievcians have not been under Clan occupation for
long . The LIC has identified rebellious elements in Krievci,
and a little independent operation (yours) may be enough
to install a new regime . You will be inserted on-planet to
discreetly assist the rebels . Equipment will be sent with you for
the rebels . You will assist them in training with the weapons,
then protect them from any retaliation .
Lyran intel is certain that no significant Clan force is in
the area . Opposition should be limited to paramilitary forces .
Most of which the rebels, with their new equipment, should
be capable of handling . If the opposition has any armored
vehicles or ’Mechs, that’s your job .
LIC estimates that after six months, the rebels should
have overwhelming control of the system and be able to open
negotiations to join the Lyran Commonwealth . |   |   | KRIEVCI DUCAL
RETAINER
Contract #: 11001156B
Employer: Duke Stephen Sabor of Krievci
Duke Sabor is the type of employer
most mercenaries drool over . He’s a Lyran
noble, which means he’s got as many
business interests as landholdings (or
more) . In Sabor’s case, he has many more
business interests, as his landholdings were
conquered by Falcons and Horses over a
decade ago . And now he wants to use that
money to fix that problem .
Location (Primary Terrain): Krievci, Former
Clan Jade Falcon Occupation Zone (Desert)
Type of Action: Retainer
Length of Contract: 6 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: 0% (1)
Transportation: 0% (5)
Salvage Rights: 100% (13)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
The contract is listed as a retainer,
which is generally defined as working for
the employer to take care of any needs
he has on his own territory . But Krievci
has been under the Clan Jade Falcon
Occupation Zone for over a decade .
Reading the fine print, the contract
claims that Krievci is no longer under
Clan control, and the Duke requires the
services of a mercenary force to protect
his holdings from insurgency .
Sounds like a fancy way of saying
you are to show up and be the Duke’s
big stick . Six months of service will earn
some serious money, if you don’t mind
knocking some skulls . |

### Table 521

| MASSASAUGA
RATTLER
Contract #: 12099834A
Employer: Duchess Cybil Bandyopadhyay
Duchess Cybil Bandyopadhyay, regent of Clermont
II within the Vesper Marches, was installed and endorsed
by the government of Vedet Brewer . A native Lyran,
her family’s rule of Clermont II stems from their family
business interest—a local factory branch of Red Devil
Industries . The Bandyopadhyays were quick to retake
their prior family business with the departure of the
Falcons, waiting to regain their proprietorship of the
Red Devil factories on Clermont II since long ago
investing in establishing an offshoot of the corporation
in 3053 . Recently they have been gradually retooling
and making further investments in light combat
vehicle manufacturing for export, providing welcome
employment for the inhabitants of Clermont II .
Location (Primary Terrain): Mississauga, Lyran
Commonwealth (Grasslands)
Type of Action: Raid
Length of Contract: 4–6 months
Base Pay: 100% (7)
Support: Straight 100% (6)
Transportation: 75% (8)
Salvage Rights: 20% (5)
Command Rights: Liaison (8)
Duchess Cybil has posted a contract with
generous terms seeking mercenaries for “Escort
Duties .” The Duchess is seeking to enforce another
ancestral claim to retake the throne of Kent Province
on Mississauga . Her contract comes with terms
offering support from her family business, Red Devil
Industries, in the form of light combat vehicles .
Her forces require protection as they seek
to establish a landhold on Mississauga, from
which they can build a forward base to project a
campaign to seize the palace on Mississauga . The
local Lyran Graf of Kent Province, Archibald Perez,
does not recognize her claims . The Duchess aims to
secure a landing zone from whence she can begin
a campaign, methodically targeting industrial hubs
before moving on the palace itself . |   | HOME GUARD
Contract #: 12099834B
Employer: Graf Archibald Perez
Graf Archibald Perez inherited his rule of
Kent Province by birth . Although the planet has
stagnated somewhat along with the rest of the Lyran
Commonwealth in recent years, Graf Perez has been
happy to maintain the status quo . The recent exploratory
activity in the region due to the departure of the Jade
Falcon forces has, however, provided a welcome
economic boom for the agricultural industry—which
the Graf has been quite pleased to take credit for .
His need to raise forces in defense of the claims
of Duchess Cybil has been unwelcome and unpleasant
for him . However, seemingly out of nowhere has come
support of war matériel from the most unexpected of
sources—the Quikscell Company . Unexpected, but not
unwelcome .
Location (Primary Terrain): Mississauga, Lyran
Commonwealth (Grasslands)
Type of Action: Raid
Length of Contract: 6 months
Base Pay: 110% (8)
Support: Battle / 20% (8)
Transportation: 50% (7)
Salvage Rights: 40% (7)
Command Rights: Integrated (3)
Graf Archibald has offered a contract with
generous payment terms, yet dubious terms of
Command rights . The Graf seeks to defend his lands
in Kent Province on the continent of Thames . He
is not entirely sure where the Duchess will strike
and requires a more mobile force of ’Mechs than
his militia .
While Graf Archibald has had negligible
response to his requests for assistance from
Tharkad in dealing with Duchess Cybil’s claims to
his province, he has found sudden and unexpected
support from the most unlikely of places—the
Quikscell Company . Quikscell is offering to
supplement the Graf’s forces in defense of his
existing rule . Graf Archibald is more than happy to
accept any support he can get—no time to inspect
the horse’s mouth . |

### Table 544

| MBEKI ALLIANCE
INVASION
Contract #: 5681123A
Employer: Watch Commander Nkrumah Mbeki
Nkrumah is one of over two dozen different rivals
currently vying for control on the planet Mkuranga . The
patriarch of his family and extended house, Nkrumah
has been waging war and cutting deals with other
departments and civil leaders in an effort to take the
mantle of leadership of the planet . Nkrumah has been
able to use his cult of personality to not only become
the dominant faction on-planet but also has been able
to turn the resources under his control into cash faster,
filling his coffers and giving him the opportunity to hire
outside forces to help cement his efforts .
Location (Primary Terrain): Mkuranga, Former Clan
Jade Falcon Occupation Zone (Wooded)
Type of Action: Invasion
Length of Contract: 12 months
Base Pay: 50% (1)
Support: Battle / 40% (10)
Transportation: 0% (5)
Salvage Rights: 80% (11)
Command Rights: Independent (11)
Watch Commander Mbeki feels that his rise to
power is imminent . It just needs that last push to
get over the edge . He’s looking for the presence of
’Mech forces to help get him over the top against
his opposition and to show his alliance with other,
leaders that his claims to control of the world
have legitimacy . The mercenaries must
find and destroy the enemies of Watch
Captain Mbeki . |   | JENGO COLLECTIVE
INVASION
Contract #: 5681123B
Employer: Analyst Commander Tafari Jengo
While Nkrumah Mbeki may be the most powerful
on Mkuranga, he is far from the only one jockeying
for dominance . Analyst Commander Tafari Jengo
leads another of the many small factions on the
planet . Following a similar path of his competitor,
Jengo is creating a loose collective to oppose Mbeki’s
push for control . Jengo is hoping to leverage the
untapped resources of the regions he controls . Along
with this, he has entered into a shaky agreement
with the Alyina Mercantile League, trading access to
resources in exchange for financial backing in the
hiring of mercenaries to assist his powerplay . This has
created an odd arrangement where the money to pay
the mercenaries the commander hires comes from
another source, and on time payments are not always
guaranteed .
As a leader, Jengo has proven himself adept
at making a lot out of little . With only infantry and
conventional forces, Jengo’s troops have been able to
secure key locations, giving him a position of bargaining
strength . That said, while a capable military commander,
Jengo is not a strong diplomat . The alliances and
partnerships he’s forged are adequate but unlikely to
stand a concerted push from the opposition . Jengo
knows this is likely the case and is hoping the hiring of
’Mech forces and the backing of the AML will help him
secure a quick victory . This would allow him to satisfy
the demands of those who’ve hitched their rifles to his
efforts at the expense of those he’s conquered .
Location (Primary Terrain): Mkuranga, Former Clan
Jade Falcon Occupation Zone (Wooded)
Type of Action: Invasion
Length of Contract: 12 months
Base Pay: 130% (10)
Support: Straight / 60% (4)
Transportation: 25% (6)
Salvage Rights: 20% (5)
Command Rights: Integrated (3)
Analyst Commander Jengo knows that if he
doesn’t act swiftly in countering the efforts of his
rival, Watch Commander Mbeki, things may not
end well for him or his allies . The mercenary will
need to destroy the rival’s military forces and allow
Jengo’s forces to take control . |

Contract #: 404495321B Length of Contract: 3 months
Employer: Lyran Commonwealth Base Pay: 100% (7)

**Page 1:**

SEEK YOUR FORTUNE!
Enter the Hinterlands with this comprehensive campaign guide to some of the hottest combat zones in the ilClan
era! A spiritual successor to the classic Hot Spots duology, this sourcebook offers all the rules and contracts any unit
needs for their ongoing BattleTech games.
Whether defending Alyina alongside the Lone Wolves, standing with a Lyran independence movement, pillaging
your way from rags to riches, or fighting to bring order to the chaos, the opportunities for enterprising commanders
are endless!
Bring the excitement of the ilClan era to your BattleTech campaign with this sourcebook, which includes complete
campaign rules built out of the BattleTech: Mercenaries boxed set, as well as contracts and game hooks for battles
throughout the Hinterlands.
FOR USE WITH

**Page 11:**

INTRODUCTION
19 September 3151—Well, as they say, every time I think I’m out, they pull me back in . “They” in this case being
my creditors, my ex’s alimony, and my bar tab . I’ll put a new crew together, snap up a contract, and score some of the
fast cash that’s sloshing around .
11 November 3151—The hiring hall has become a boom town, as far as the merc trade goes . Last time I blew
through, the whole affair fit neatly into some converted warehouses down by the port . Now there are arms dealers,
proving grounds, and the whole ecosystem of bar/pawnshop/brothel mercenary “support services” (Francine was as
friendly as ever) . I skipped the MRBC offices and headed over to the hall, which looked and smelled like a recently
converted seafood market . I had to listen to the rep’s pitch about how signing onto their covenants will usher in a new
golden age for mercenaries . I got through it thinking about Francine, then registered us as Kev’s Killers . No way I’m using
the old unit’s name—there’s a reason I skipped the MRBC offices .
13 December 3151—Took a chance on some warlord, fighting some other warlord . They’re scooping up units of
all shapes and sizes—even piddling lances like us . Pay’s hefty, and nothing in the contract requires I do anything stupid:
beyond the showing up to fight for a warlord part . Rounded up the crew and told ’em to get their ’Mechs ready for
transit . We ship out into the unknown tomorrow at dawn . Maybe we’ll hit the big time and make our reps . Maybe we’ll
get crushed by the Horses or blown up by insurgents . Hell, worst comes to worst, I can expense the whole mess as a
business write-off, call it field research, and kick out a new edition of my book .
Hinterlands, here we come .
—Personal Journal, Kevan “Kronerhound” Ortman
HOW TO USE THIS BOOK
Hot Spots Hinterlands is a campaign sourcebook for running a
player created mercenary force in the ilClan era . Players create their
own mercenary command, select and negotiate contracts at
various hot spot locations, and then play out those battles .
They maintain their force and hopefully expand and
improve the force as they successfully complete contracts .
The rules in this volume are based on the A Game of
Armored Combat, Clan Invasion, and BattleTech: Mercenaries
box sets . The Battlefield Support rules from the BattleMech
Manual or BattleTech: Mercenaries will be useful for combined arms
options . Alternatively, the players may use the Alpha Strike rules from
Alpha Strike: Commander’s Edition for their campaign .
Players can use Total Warfare for combined arms vehicles and
infantry, and they can use A Time of War or MechWarrior: Destiny for
role-playing their mercenary campaigns, but neither of those options
are assumed for Hot Spots Hinterlands .
Thor (Summoner) T—Gray Gunny Lancers

MMEERRCCNNEETT
41 Hinterlands Status January 3151–March 3151
74 Alyina: Go straight to the source for the best equipment a merc—or pirate—can get.
Whether it’s defending the factory or stealing from it.
78 Hinterlands Status April 3151–June 3151
80 Apostica: The meek will inherit, until the neighbor decides to pay a visit. Can a local
government hold off the strong-arm tactics of a neighboring system.
84 Benfled: The prey is on the run. Is the trap for the hunted or the hunter?
87 Hinterlands Status July 3151–September 3151
88 Chahar: Faith and industry collide to shape the future of Chahar.
92 Esteros: Your mine is my mine. Control of the mines will determine the fate of Esteros,
but mostly get you paid well.
96 Evciler: A merchant needs some leverage. You need a horse trailer?
97 Hinterlands Status October 3151–December 3151
101 Krievci: The Duke is ready to reclaim his land, but what if they already have their own
plans? Not all Lyrans are happy to welcome back their former government.
105 Mississauga: New opportunities bring about a chance to settle old disagreements
between rival nobles.
109 Mkuranga: The forewarned Watch made Mkuranga a safe haven in the Hinterlands,
until the popular leader dies in mysterious circumstances.
113 Hinterlands Status January 3152–March 3152
114 Mogyorod: The Horses are racing, and the host wants to show them a good time.
116 Summit: When the going gets tough, the people start hoarding gold in convenient
shipping containers.
120 Toland: Of course there’s a pirate map to buried loot, surrounded by implacable
guardians. At least there’s no snakes?
123 Hinterlands Status April 3152–June 3152
124 Wotan: The local scrappers have turned up something very interesting, and competing
interests look to secure the scrap.
128 Acts of Piracy: Why waste time on the boring details and contracts, just grab the
loot and profit.

SKILLS
BattleTech: Each ’Mech starts with 4 Gunnery and 5
Piloting, but you start with two Skill Advances to improve
(lower) your starting Skills . You cannot apply more than one
Skill Advance to a single Skill at this point . You can lower your
Gunnery and Piloting by one each, or you can lower your
Gunnery by one and your friend’s (if you got a second ’Mech)
Gunnery Skill by one, and so on .
Alpha Strike: Each ’Mech starts with Skill 4, and you start
with one Skill Advance to lower one unit’s starting Skill .
BATTLEFIELD SUPPORT
BattleField Support rules add air support, artillery
support, and other non-’Mech military hardware . The
BattleMech Manual and Alpha Strike: Commander’s Edition
contain the initial rules for BattleField Support, and the new
Mercenaries box set updates and expands these rules . If you
are using the older BattleMech Manual rules, double the BSP
costs listed in those rulebooks . If using the Mercenaries box
set updated costs, there is no modification needed .
In addition to your ’Mechs, you have available an
allotment of Battlefield Support . You receive 32 Battlefield
Support Points (BSP) each track (5 in AS) . You also receive any
unused BV of your 3,000 BV converted to additional BSP, at a
rate of 20 unused BV to 1 BSP (round down) . You can convert
up to 640 BV to +32 BSP, at Scale 1 (see Scale, below) . This is
calculated each track; compare the forces you are bringing
to the BV limit of 3,000 BV and receive the remaining unused
BV to BSP for that track . Note that the terms “Battlefield” and
“Support” are both used in a variety of ways in these rules .
However, only when they are both together (i .e ., Battlefield
Note that as you gain or lose units from your force each

SCALE
As you progress in your mercenary campaign, you
may, hopefully, find your mercenary command growing
into an actual company or even larger (see Changing
Scale, p . 31) . We track this as the Scale of the force . Your
initial Scale is 1 .
16 BECOMING A MERCENARY
EXAMPLE Arthur is ready for a mercenary campaign. He
wants a strong, tough unit for his commander, and he
goes with the Caesar CES-3R at 1,587 BV. This gives him
1,413 BV remaining. He would like something faster for
grabbing loot, scanning, or other objectives. He takes
an Ostsol OTL-5M at 1,245 BV. The remaining 168 BV
is converted to 8 additional Battlefield Support Points.
Added to the initial 32 BSP gives Arthur 40 BSP total for
the first track.
After reviewing the Battlefield Support rules and
cards, Arthur plans for a pair of Galleon GAL-100 assets at
15 BSP each and a Light Air Strike at 10 BSP. The Galleons
can either perform reconnaissance, support the Ostsol in
quick strikes or maneuver into the rear of any tough unit
threatening the Caesar. The Air Strike can be called in to
finish off enemy units or objectives as needed.
Arthur has two Skill Advances to apply. He assigns
one to lower the Caesar’s Gunnery from 4 to 3. Precise
firepower is the dream of any mercenary. The second Skill
Advance is used to lower the Ostsol’s Piloting from 5 to
4. This will hopefully allow the Ostsol to get in and out.
Arthur marks down the 3,000 SP as the beginning
WarChest, the initial Scale of 1, and the initial Reputation
of 1 on the Mercenary Force Record sheet. The final step is
naming his mercenary company, and the Henshin Tigers
have registered as a new mercenary command!
Arthur’s mercenary life continues on page 20 as he
negotiates his first contract.

Chaos Campaign: Mercenaries play is built around contracts, to set the general terms of the upcoming conflict and the terms
of payment for the mercenary . After accepting a contract, each month may generate one of the available tracks (a game session)
for the terms of that battle . Each track provides a general framework to help players create the Attacking and Defending forces
and add battlefield conditions and objectives .

% Force: The term “force” refers to the total collection of units that a given
side has available . SIMPLE

which the mercenary achieves a higher total points award from completing

Negative Reputation has no effect other than to require the mercenary to gain more Reputation in order to reach positive effects .
CONTRACTS
The heart of surviving as a mercenary is the contract . Any month you do not have a contract, including the beginning of
the campaign, you may search for what contracts are offered to you . The Hot Spots section of this book (see pp . 74–130) includes
fourteen hot spots, each with at least one contract . The player may decide from these which they wish to play . In addition, there are
optional rules in the Almotacen Hiring Hall section (see pp . 58–70) with rules for creating random contracts that may be available .
GENERAL CONTRACT TERMS
Base Pay: Most contracts have a Base Payment that is paid every month based on an average of 500 SP per month . This is
paid each month under contract, including transit times, while in garrison, training, repairing, etc . The Base Pay is based on and
provided to cover the maintenance costs of the mercenaries and includes salaries, the upkeep and care of personnel, and the
PLAYING A CHAOS CAMPAIGN 17

**Page 20:**

HOT SPOTS HINTERLANDS
upkeep and care of the equipment . Any shortfalls in Base Pay, from the employer paying less than 100 percent Base Pay, need to
be covered by the players . Any overage in Base Pay, for higher Base Pay percentage or combat mission bonuses for example, are
kept by the players to pay for other costs or to spend as they see fit .
Maintenance Costs: The standard cost for maintenance is 500 SP per month, the basis for 100 percent Base Pay . This cost
needs to be paid by the player even if not under contract; for example, if they want to wait a month in the hopes of a better
contract offer, they pay the maintenance cost each month they wait for the “right” contract . The maintenance cost for higher
Scale forces is multiplied by the Scale . A Scale 3 force pays 1,500 SP per month in
maintenance costs, and 100 percent Base Pay for that force would be 1,500 SP .
Combat Pay: Money that is paid upon completion of a track . Combat
payment is based upon 500 SP for a successfully completed track, multiplied

Repair Costs: These costs are detailed in Time Between Tracks (see p . 29)
and include any SP Activity listed as repair, heal, or rearm . Repair costs are
covered by the Support terms of a mercenary contract . If the Support term lists
Straight / 100% or Battle with any percentage, then all repair costs are covered in their
entirety . A Support term of Straight with a percentage will cover that percentage of repair costs .
Battlefield Losses: These costs include any SP Activity listed as purchase or hire if they are used to replace a destroyed unit
(of the same type) . If the Support term lists Battle with a percentage, then that percentage of Battlefield Loss costs are paid by the
employer . The employer will pay the percentage of the cost to purchase the lost unit .
Salvage: Salvage rights cover the equipment recovered from the enemy . Salvage is valued at its selling price, then the salvage
rights percentage is applied to divide it as closely as possible to percentage rights (see Repairs, Purchasing, Salvage, and Other
Expenses, p . 29) . If the salvage rights are listed as Exchange, the mercenary gets none of the salvage but gets paid one-quarter the
salvage value instead . The SP from Exchange rights must be used for repair costs this month or is lost .

CAMPAIGNING IN THE HINTERLANDS
Command: Command Rights determine how much control the employer exerts over the command decisions of the
mercenaries . Independent command is a true freelancer: do the job and get the pay . Liaison means an official is detached to
represent the employer with the mercenaries and monitor their work and verify the mercenaries’ actions . House command means
the mercenaries are placed under the employer’s command: they will detail when, where, and how the mercenaries will attack .
But it is still at the level of the commander of the mercenaries’ answers to the House Command . The employer must follow the
chain of command . Finally, Integrated command means the employer has practically full command, where the mercenaries’ full
force is considered to be members of the employer’s command and must individually follow any orders given by the employer .
The employer may give orders to individual lances or mercenaries if they choose to do so . The effects of Command Rights will be
detailed in the contract’s Mission Briefing .
NEGOTIATING A CONTRACT
The contract will have default payment and support terms . The player may negotiate some changes to these terms . For every
point of Reputation (initially 1), the player may increase one payment or Support type by
one step (see Contract Steps Table, p . 20) . The amount of Reputation that can be applied
to a contract is a maximum of twice the Scale of the contract . At the initial Scale of
CAMPAIGN
1, up to 2 Reputation can be used to modify a contract . Each player negotiates their

Once the negotiations are complete, the player has an
accepted contract . The Mission Briefing for the contract will then list how
to generate tracks with the contract .
Each Mission Briefing will list the Intensity, or number of tracks to
be played during the contract . This may be a set number or a random
number . The track will determine the number of mapsheets and
objectives for the track . There may be Special Rules that modify the
track, including the effects of Command Rights .
Contract Type: The type is nonnegotiable but is a broad, general
description of what work the contract is expected to entail . Many
of the contract terms are directly or indirectly related to the type
of contract, and the contract type should be kept in mind when
negotiating the terms . Battle loss compensation is likely far more
valuable in an invasion contract than in a raid contract .

HOT SPOTS HINTERLANDS
% Raid: A raid contract is an offensive contract with the
mercenary hired to strike the enemy to damage them
or take something from them . A typical raid is an
objective raid, to claim valuable cargo from the enemy .
A diversionary raid is similar, but the intent of the strikes
is merely to force the enemy to reposition to defend from
the strikes . A pirate raid is similar to an objective raid,
only the goal is to take and sell the cargo rather than
acquire the cargo for their employer .
% Expedition: An expedition contract is an offensive
contract with the mercenary hired to go to an enemy
system and acquire intelligence and/or assist intelligence
operations in the system . A reconnaissance mission
determines the distribution of forces on the system and
the location of important assets . Intelligence support
operations might entail delivering supplies to insurgents
or intelligence agents or recovering information from
those agents . A guerrilla operation may seem like a raid,
but rather than a quick operation to acquire goods, a
guerrilla operation is intended to last a longer time, and
the goal is to tie up the enemy rather than specifically
cause damage .
% Pirate Hunt: A pirate hunt is a hybrid of expedition and
raid, where the mercenary must hunt down the location
of the pirates and then strike at their base to disrupt or
even destroy their operations .
% Garrison: A garrison contract is a defensive contract
to defend the employer’s land or
assets . Cadre Duty is similar, but
rather than the expectation that

20 PLAYING A CHAOS CAMPAIGN
EXAMPLE
Arthur’s mercenaries take their first mercenary
contract, the Great Eagle Garrison. He uses the one
term step bump from his Reputation of 1 to increase
the Base Pay from 100 percent (7) to 110 percent (8).
He is a little uncertain about giving up any Command
Rights, but the contract already has no support rights,
and he’s unwilling to give up salvage rights to remove
the liaison requirement. It is all about the money, and
the Henshin Tigers have accepted the first contract.
Arthur pays for his first month’s maintenance,
500 SP. He also collects his travel month’s pay. The
base is 500 SP, but the contract pays 110 percent, 550
SP. He spends the first month traveling to the contract
location, but the contract covers 100 percent (it would
have cost 300 SP, but the contract pays 300 SP of it).
All clear so far!
Having arrived at the contract location, Arthur
moves to the first month of the contract. He pays this
month’s maintenance, which is again 100 percent
covered by the contract plus 50 SP, and he is feeling
good about negotiating that Base Pay increase. He has
earned 100 SP just traveling to the contract location!

GAME SETUP
The Defender selects a short edge as their home edge, and the Attacker gets the opposite short edge as their home edge . The
Defender designates (or places if the map has no appropriate buildings already) 4 Medium buildings within 3 hexes (AS: 6”) of the
center of the battlefield . The buildings take 30 damage before being destroyed and are immobile (−4 target number modifier) .
ATTACKER
The Attacker enters half of their force from their home edge at the start of the track . The remainder of the force enters from
an edge perpendicular to the home edge during the Movement Phase of turn 2 .
DEFENDER
The Defender deploys their entire force prior to Initiative anywhere on their half of the battlefield . At least 50 percent must
be within 3 hexes (AS: 6”) of the designated buildings . Secretly designate one of these buildings as headquarters before the start
of the track .
OBJECTIVES
Identify and Destroy (Attacker only): Locate (using Scanning, see below) and destroy the headquarters building . [200]

Seek and Destroy (Defender only): Destroy/Cripple the Commander . [200]

TRACK END

Alpha Strike) .
SCANNING
Buildings may be scanned to determine if they are
the objective . BattleMechs and vehicles may declare
a scan on a target building within 2 hexes or 4 hexes
if the unit has an Active Probe (AS: 4” or 8” if the
unit has the PRB special) during the Weapon Attack
Phase, and the results of the scan are determined
during the End Phase of the same turn, provided
the unit remains functioning and does not
have any sensor critical hits . A unit with an
Active Probe may fire weapons during
the same turn as scanning, otherwise
the unit can only scan . Battle armor
and infantry may scan a building if
they are adjacent to or inside the
building . They may not attack the
same turn they scan .
When a building is scanned,
the Defender reveals whether
it is the headquarters objec-
tive or not .

JagerMech—Pirate forces
have one lance of three ’Mechs and another of four but
cannot have two lances of three ’Mechs or a lance of three
and a lance of five . The player does not have to field
the entire lance in a track, but if a Formation has less
than three units fielded during gameplay—whether
from not fielding all the units, units being destroyed
in combat, or a combination—it loses the benefits of that
Formation .
CHANGING SCALE
If the player has enough ’Mechs and has built up the
Support Points for a larger force and wants to play larger
missions, they may increase the Scale they are operating at
(advertise as a larger force or look for bigger contracts) . This
increases their Scale by 1 . This increases the size force (BV
limit) and support (BSP) they can bring to each track .
Meeting Your Opponent at their Scale: If one player is
at a lower Scale, the higher Scale player must choose a force
for this track following the limits of the lower Scale . If a Scale
2 player is playing against a Scale 1 player, the Scale 2 player
must build their force for this track limiting themselves to
Scale 1 (3,000 BV and 32 BSP) . The bonus pay for this track is
paid at the Scale played .
Battles Between Different Advancements: Each Skill
improvement for a MechWarrior or Special Pilot Ability is an
advancement . For every difference of more than 2 between
two players’ total Advancements, the lower advancement
force gets 3 additional BSP . Pay for this track is paid at the
Scale played .
WARCHEST DEBT
Players may not go into Warchest Debt to pay for more
If the players do not have enough SPs to pay
than 6 months without paying off the debt . If a force is already
maintenance or transportation, they may go into Warchest
in debt and still cannot pay for maintenance or transportation,
Debt to continue operating .
there are three options:
Players may not purchase services and equipment or
improve their MechWarriors with Warchest Debt; the option % Sell Assets: The first option is to sell assets until the force
may only be used to cover maintenance and transportation . can pay off its debt . Note that repairs while in debt are
Any SPs gained after incurring debt are used to pay off not permitted, so only units that are already completely
the debt until the WarChest regains a positive balance . undamaged may be sold .

HOT SPOTS HINTERLANDS
% Retire: Second, the player may decide that
the force is now retired, and the campaign
ends . This may be the appropriate response
if selling off assets would reduce the force to
such a degree that future tracks are not likely
to be fun for the player . Keeping it fun must
always be the goal .
% Skip a Track: The third option is to have the
force skip a track . This is only possible while
you are in debt . The force spends its time
repairing, refitting, and arranging for the
next track . Only repairs may be executed;
no additional activities may be performed
during a skipped track, even if there is SP
available . The players lose 1 Reputation from
avoiding the track . The players play the next
track of the contract or begin searching for a
new contract if they are out of contract . The
players are considered one lower Command
Rights level (from Liaison to House, from
House to Integrated) for this track . The option
to skip a track may only be taken once; if the
force skips a track, plays a following track, and
afterward again cannot afford a subsequent
track, they must either sell assets or retire .
BREAKING CONTRACT
Sometimes it is obvious that continuing
the contract is untenable for one player . Not
having enough units to form anywhere near a
Scale 1 (3,000 BV) force for the next track would
be the most obvious example . If the player sees
no hope in fighting the next track, they may
opt to break the contract . Breaking contract
is normally a −3 Reputation . If the player has
less than two-thirds of the Scale limit, less than
2,000 BV remaining at Scale 1, this is only a
−1 Reputation penalty by invoking an escape
clause . The contract ends immediately for the
mercenary breaking contract . They no longer
get support, Base Pay, etc . for the remainder
of the contract . The player may then decide if
they want to continue playing this mercenary
command or start a new command .
The other player, no longer having an oppo-
nent, gains Reputation for having successfully
completed the contract . They continue to get
support, Base Pay, etc . from the contract for the
remaining time of the contract but no bonus pay,
since they don’t actually face combat .
32 TIME BETWEEN TRACKS
EXAMPLE Arthur’s mercenaries take their first mercenary contract,
Esteros Disruption. He uses the one term step bump from his
Reputation of 1 to increase the Base Pay from 70% (4) to 80% (5).
He decides he also does not care about Command Rights from
Independent (11) to the next marked level, Liaison (8). Arthur can
use two of those to increase Base Pay yet again, from 80% (5) to
90% (6). It is all about the money, and the Henshin Tigers have
accepted the first contract.
Arthur pays for his first month’s maintenance, 500 SP. He also
collects his travel month’s pay. The base is 500 SP, but the contract
pays 90 percent, 450 SP. He spends the first month traveling to the
contract location at 300 SP, and the contract covers nothing. Arthur
needs a fight, because he’s paid out 350 SP so far.
Having arrived at the contract location, Arthur moves to the
first month of the contract. He pays this month’s maintenance,
which is again 90 percent covered by the contract, so owes
another 50 SP.
Arthur then looks over the GM’s details for the Esteros
Disruption contract. The track is randomly rolled and comes up as
Strike. Arthur plays the track out with his force and a friend plays
theirs as the opposing force. After setting up the track, Arthur rolls
for command complications and finds his Liaison Command Rights
gave his opponent some extra tanks. But hopefully the money will
be worth it.
Arthur’s Ostsol successfully locates the headquarters for
the Strike objective and destroys it. He then withdraws his forces.
Having achieved one objective (locate and destroy the HQ) and
managing not to get his force destroyed or his commander
destroyed, he has 200 WP from objectives versus 0 for the opposing
force. This is a successful track! Arthur adds one to his Reputation
for a successful track. He completed the one and only objective, and
the opponent did not complete any objectives, so Arthur gets a 150
percent combat bonus, 750 SP! This successful track also entitles
Arthur to collect salvage. Arthur only has 70 percent salvage rights
and only destroyed or crippled a single enemy ’Mech, the Flea-21 at
826 BV. A Flea’s selling price is half it’s BV, for 413 SP. Arthur collects
289 SP for his salvage rights, and then he can choose to pay the
124 SP to keep the (crippled) Flea. Arthur’s not so sure the Flea is
what he wanted and just keeps the 289 SP.
The Ostsol took some internal and armor damage, and
Caesar took armor damage. The Ostsol was not crippled, but took
internal damage, and costs 60 tons × 2 to repair. 120 SP in repairs
are planned for the Ostsol. The Caesar only needs armor repair,
but it is 70 tons / 2 for 35 SP. He also wants to rearm one ton of
ammunition at 10 SP each for another 10 SP. Arthur needs 165 SP
to do the planned repair/rearm tasks.
He now has his initial 3,000 SP, paid 400 SP in maintenance
and travel, plus 750 for track bonus, gained 289 from selling
salvage, and paid 165 for repairs and rearming, for a new total
of 3,474 SP. Arthur is ready for month two of paying the bills and
fighting the fight.

There are two options for a Clan Hell’s Horses player to
purchase and use combat vehicles: as Battlefield Support
REGULAR MILITARY Assets or as standard units . Battlefield Support Assets tend to
be simpler and slightly less tough but are cheaper to field and
RANK TABLE
simpler to maintain . Standard units have more complexity,
Reputation Scale Rank including being able to train Skills and pilot abilities, but must
be maintained as units and can be destroyed permanently .

are found in Total Warfare .

Archon Trillian Steiner inherited a battered and broken
Lyran Commonwealth in 3143, having lost dozens of star
systems and public confidence in House Steiner’s ability to
lead . The Buena Collective seceded in 3146, followed by the
Timbuktu Collective in 3148 . Fearing her entire realm would
fly apart if she devoted her limited resources to reclaiming the
Hinterlands, Trillian relied on Operation Black Ice, sending Loki
terrorist teams to dozens of worlds for insurgency support
but holding her military in reserve . Such hesitation let others
seize the initiative .

CLAN EMPLOYERS
In the Hinterlands proper, the first Clan to breach
the taboo against hiring mercenaries was the Alyina
Mercantile League, which saw them as a temporary
solution to an existential crisis. Seeking to defend
themselves without accidentally re-creating a dominant
warrior caste, they sent agents to hiring halls, offering
cadre contracts to help raise and train an army from the
League’s freeborn population.
Jiyi Chistu’s Falcons did not seek mercenary support
but accepted the Lone Wolves’ help when the Horses
attacked Sudeten. Chistu handsomely rewarded the
unit for its bravery and willingness to fight in the Falcons’
defense even without a formal contract. They may be
open to adding additional units to their roster, if any can

HIRING HALL PROFILE: ALMOTACEN
APOLLINAIRE MALTHUS
A consummate survivor, Apollinaire Malthus is the
ambitious visionary behind Almotacen’s new life . As patriarch
of a junior branch of the family, Apollinaire once presided
over several lucrative gambling and smuggling operations
on Dustball under the Jade Falcon occupation before internal
power struggles exposed his operations to the unwelcome
attention of the warrior caste . He barely escaped with his
life and took refuge with a band of his pirate suppliers
on Almotacen to plot his return to power . Upon learning
of the sudden disappearance of the Jade Falcon Touman,
Apollinaire spied a chance to turn his meager power base into
something grander . Leveraging his authority as the planet’s
chief fence, he suggested a series of deliberately brazen raids
to the pirates in order to bring Almotacen to the attention
of potentially more lucrative allies while quietly putting out
feelers to neighboring planets advertising a neutral ground
should the pirates be removed . The arrival of the Peregrine
Lancers served his purposes perfectly .
Apollinaire deliberately presents himself as a genial and
slightly doddering old man, but years in exile on Almotacen
have not dulled his wrath or his opportunism one whit . He is

HOT SPOTS OF THE HINTERLANDS
THE MERCENARIES’ GUILD
From 2956 to 2968, the mercenaries of the Inner Sphere
attempted to unionize, banding together to form a guild.
This was a response to a rash of mistreatment of mercenary
forces by their employers. Some Successor States entrapped
commands in debt through intentional “company store”
mechanisms, locking units into overpriced supply contracts
that resulted in the mercenaries effectively paying their
employers for the privilege of service. Others considered
their mercenaries to be expendable, throwing their lives
away on suicidal diversionary gambits. ComStar’s Mercenary
Review Board consistently ruled in favor of employers, feeding
growing unrest in the mercenary community.
The guild required that employers agree to restrictive
covenants in all contracts, primarily seeking to revert battlefield
conduct (especially between guild members) to the traditions
of the long-abandoned Ares Conventions. Mercenaries
operating under the covenant focused on maneuver more
than kill tallies, allowed battles to be decided by Solaris-style
duels between unit champions, and included strict provisions
on prisoner repatriation and the ransom of equipment.
Though popular in concept with the mercenary community,
it was sabotaged by ComStar’s covert machinations and the
Successor States’ practice of offering contract sweeteners in
exchange for waiving the covenant. Appearing impotent
in the face of continued employer misconduct, the guild
broke up acrimoniously. ComStar’s Mercenary Review Board
regained its primacy, holding sway over the mercenary market
until being replaced by the Mercenary Review and Bonding
Commission (MRBC).
In 3132, the Blackout destroyed the MRBC’s business
model. After declaring bankruptcy, it relaunched under new
leadership in 3133, taking a blatantly pro-employer stance.
Despite the surging demand for mercenaries, units trying to
work through the MRBC found themselves barely able to make
ends meet. In 3145, mercenary activists proposed resurrecting
the Mercenaries’ Guild.
The guild offers members insurance policies, employer
matchmaking services, professional contract negotiators,
arbitrations, payment escrows, and binding agreements that
guild members will adhere to the Ares Conventions when
battling each other. It coordinates blacklists against employers
that mistreat its members, and it promises retaliation against
members that break the covenant. We are now accepting
members Sphere-wide.
—the hiNterlANds,
MerCenaries’ Guild MeMber advisory, 3152
Ostroc—Helmut’s Legion

APOSTICA MISSION BRIEF (CONT .)
SPECIAL RULES
Victory: Both contracts automatically conclude after all five tracks are played . Whichever side has successfully
completed more tracks is the victor on Apostica .
Defense Track: If using Battlefield Support Assets, the following complication may be added to the Defend Track
(5) . In addition to any that may be added to the track through the Command Complication table, each side receives three
unarmed Foot Infantry Battlefield Support Assets during the Defense track . These follow the same rules as listed above .
Before the movement phase, a player may immediately remove one of the opponent’s Foot Infantry Support assets by
offering them a spot on their DropShip . This results in a −1 Reputation penalty .
If a player chooses to remove all three of their opponent’s special Foot Infantry Battlefield Support Assets from
play, they immediately forfeit the track and are considered in breach of contract . This breach results in no additional
Reputation loss, and the player still receives combat pay as if they had successfully completed the track with all
objectives as the grateful Aposticans pay the mercenaries with their savings .
Temporary Hire: The first time a mercenary force is unable to field a force of at least two-thirds of the maximum
permissible BV/PV for their next track, a local mercenary will offer their services . The player may choose each track of
this contract whether they want to pay the local mercenary to participate in each track or not .
Sand Wyrm: Abandoned after a failed Kuritan raid, the ancient Sand Wyrm offers his services to those who help
keep his wind-scarred ’Mech functional . Sand Wyrm pilots a Hatamoto-Chi HTM-27T (1,607 BV / 39 PV) and has Piloting
3, Gunnery 4 (AS: Skill 3) . Sand Wyrm demands 50 WarChest Points per track that he participates in and a bonus 50
WarChest Points for each unit he destroys .
PURCHASE OPTIONS
’Mechs are exceedingly rare on Apostica, though both sides are able to access limited numbers
of ancient, abandoned machines . At the end of each track, roll 2D6 . On a 9+ there is
a single ’Mech for sale; on a 12+ there are two .
Roll 1D6, once for each ’Mech for sale, on the Apostica Random Allocation
Table to determine which ’Mech is available for purchase . This ’Mech is available
until the end of the contract, at which point it may no longer be purchased .

Behind the Scenes: The player with the Eliminate Koniz Falcons contract will hereafter be called Tamar . The opposing
force will be Clan Hell’s Horses . If the House/Clan Regular Military option is being used (see p . 39), then the Hell’s
Horses may be a player force .
Month 1 is spent traveling . The mercenaries arrive in system and chase the fleeing DropShip to the planet
surface . They watch as their quarry comes into contact with a Hell’s Horses force and is defeated . The Jade Falcon
commander is taken as bondsman with their ’Mech . The players must destroy that ’Mech to fulfill their contract .
Month 2 begins with the first track, a Meeting Engagement . The mercenaries attack and attempt to destroy the
Jade Falcon commander . The Hell’s Horses must replace one ’Mech with Star Captain Vasilii Icaza (see Special Rules, p .
86) . This must leave them below the Scale limit for this track . The mercenaries are the Attacker .
The second track is dependent on the results of the first track . If the mercenaries did not successfully complete
the track, then Track 2 is Recon as they attempt to find where the Star
Captain is now . The mercenaries are the Attacker .

SPECIAL RULES
Victory: Both contracts automatically conclude after six tracks are played . If the sixth track is completed while more
months are left in the contracts, no further tracks are played, and the opposition concedes . Both players still receive Base
Pay for the remaining months in their contracts . The player that has successfully completed more tracks is the victor .
Temporary Hire: The first time a mercenary force is unable to field a force of at least two-thirds of the maximum
permissible BV/PV for their next track, a local mercenary will offer their services . The player may choose each track of
this contract whether they want to pay the local mercenary to participate in each track or not .
Laser Bait: Once a gladiator on Solaris VII, Paulina Ferrar left the world upon its fall to Clan Wolf . Paulina pilots a
Hellion T (1,637 BV / 42 PV) and has Piloting 4, Gunnery 3 (AS: Skill 3) . Paulina demands 50 WarChest Points per track that
she participates in and a bonus 50 WarChest Points for each unit she destroys .

marginal prospects for colonization . The proximity to its
small sun and a weak ozone layer limited its suitability for
human life despite having a Terra-like atmosphere, while the
native, purple-tinted flora were incompatible with Terran
biology . However, the presence of considerable mineral
resources drove colonization, supported by Star League
technology .

HOT SPOTS OF THE HINTERLANDS
RODEO RAID MISSION BRIEF
Contract #: 950014A
Intensity (Number of Tracks): 2–3
Behind the Scenes: There are two initial tracks in this contract, with a possible third, depending on the success of
the first two . The initial job is as stated, with no real trickery behind the scenes . Pettersson expects anonymity to be
preserved at all costs in the initial attack .
The real complications come in the acquisition and holding on to the hell’s horse . The animals are notoriously
wild and untamable, and Star Colonel David Fletcher is only able to keep his as a mascot by having an overly large
ranging area . That, and he’s discovered a local fauna the horse is quite fond of .
Pettersson also wants to avoid extensive casualties as a gesture of goodwill toward the Clan and the merchant
caste and to showcase his own feelings of superiority .
While an absolutely arrogant, Type A personality jerk, Pettersson will treat the unit fairly in negotiations . To a
point, anyway . He’s not about to let them roll over with requests and extra concessions, but he very much wants this
mission to succeed and is willing to “spend money to make money .”

Contract #: 11001156A–B

Behind the Scenes: These two contracts are opposing contracts . Both sides will be player-controlled mercenaries . The
player with the Ducal Retainer contract will be called Duke’s Own; the player with the Blue Martens Covert Operations,
Blue Martens .
Emilijia Saric knows perfectly well Duke Sabor is coming for Krievci, and she has intentionally hired the mercenaries to
assist the defense against his forces . She has been making contact with the Blue Martens of Krievci for decades, even before
the Hell’s Horses took the system from the Lyran Commonwealth, to assist them in deposing the Duke . Now they see their
opportunity to throw off both .
The Duke is also aware of the Blue Martens: they are an old nemesis of his . He knows he needs to make a move before
the Archon can negotiate with the rebels and cut him out of his landhold .
Month 1: The first month is spent in travel and training . The Blue Martens travel immediately
to Krievci, deliver the equipment and begin training the rebels . The Duke’s Own travel to
Tsinan and are given fancy dress uniforms and given extensive notes on what exactly
Nightsky
belongs to the Duke on Krievci and how they are to deal with anybody claiming —Unknown OpFor
otherwise .
Month 2: The second month, the Duke’s Own land on Krievci . Initially
the two forces avoid each other . The Duke’s Own are given explicit
directions on what to claim and hold . The Blue Martens are content
to let the Duke’s Own sit there quietly .
Month 3: The third month, the Duke demands the
Duke’s Own deal with the Blue Martens . He is concerned
that time will lead to negotiations, and he has no intention
of conceding anything on Krievci . He accuses the Blue
Martens of harboring Clan warriors and sends the
Duke’s Own to attack the ’Mechs of the Blue Martens .

month . Only armor repair and rearming

in Track 1, the Duke will order a second Assault for
the second track . Again randomly determined desert
Mapsheets, and the Duke’s Own are the Attacker .

the Blue Martens counter with an Objective Raid for the second track to
destroy the supply depot, hoping that the lack of local support will lead to
a withdrawal . Use random desert mapsheets again, but the Attacker may add
four medium buildings to the map on the Defender’s half of the play area, for the
supply depot .

MISSION BRIEF
Contract #: 12099834A–B
Intensity (Number of Tracks): 6
Behind the Scenes: These two contracts are opposing contracts . Both sides will be player-controlled mercenaries . The
player with the Massasauga Rattler contract will be called Rattler, attempting ultimately to seize Kent Palace . The player
with the Home Guard contract will be called Guard .
The first month, there is no track . The time is spent in travel and preparation .
The tracks are played in the specified order as the Duchess attempts to seize a landing zone, then defend the
Combat Depot built there . Missions #3 and #4 are optional missions the Attacker can choose to undertake, to seize
the Rail Hub and Aeroplex on the way to the palace . If neither Mission #3 nor #4 is chosen, there is no track in month
4 . Missions #5 and #6 are the final battle for the Palace of Kent .
Month 6 is spent either withdrawing from the system or consolidating the hold on the system, and there is no track .

Behind the Scenes: These two contracts are opposing contracts . Both sides will be player-controlled
mercenaries . The player with the Mbeki Alliance Invasion contract will be called Mbeki; the player with
the Jengo Collective Invasion contract, Jengo .
Note this contract has an unusual victory condition for the contract that may “end” the contract early .
For each track, each player rolls 2D6 (reroll ties) . The player with the higher total chooses to be the Attacker or the
Defender . Note this is before determining the specific track .
After the choice of Attacker/Defender, the player that successfully completed the last track, if any, may choose to
add or subtract 1 or have no modifier at all . Then roll 1D6 on the track table
to determine the track .

MOGYOROD ESCORT MISSION BRIEF
Contract #: 35559812
Intensity (Number of Tracks): 3
Behind the Scenes: This contract will pit Mercenaries against Clan Hell’s Horses . The player with the mercenary contract
will be called Mercs; and the Clan will be called Hell’s Horses . The Hell’s Horses can be played by a gamemaster (GM)
opposing force, with the force for each track being rolled on the opposing force tables on page 135, or a player can
build a Hell’s Horses force as a player force using the option on page 39 for House/Clan Regular Military force . The
Hell’s Horses player should have vehicles available, either using the Battlefield Support rules for vehicles coming in
the BattleTech: Mercenaries box set or the vehicle rules from Total Warfare .
These tracks can technically be played in any order, but once the Horses arrive on Mogyorod, they will happen
in relatively short
succession (i .e ., inside
a single month) . The TRACK TABLE
player can roll 1D6 to

COMPLICATIONS TABLE
1D6 Complication
1–3 No complications.
Demon duck mobs are attracted to the running lights on tanks and BattleMechs, confusing them for fellow sauropods or esculant fungi.
Randomly place 1D6−2 demon duck mobs on the battlefield. Each mob occupies one entire hex and is treated as a BattleMech unit for stacking
4–5 purposes. Every odd turn, the player controls each pack; every even turn, the opponent controls them; the packs always move after all other
units have moved. Demon duck packs may move two hexes in any direction; act as though they are a level 1 piece of terrain regarding LOS
through the hex they occupy, and they cannot be destroyed for game purposes.
The demon ducks are an additional objective for the players. A unit adjacent to a demon duck mob may use the Objective Raid rules for picking
up components (p. 25) in order to pick up a demon duck. No other objectives may be claimed by the player unless it has a unit carrying a demon
6+
duck component. They may fulfill other objectives (destroy units, etc.), but if they don’t have a demon duck, then the objective isn’t counted
until they do. This applies to both players.

Once one of the original members of the
Rim Worlds Republic and an
influential commercial
hub, Wotan has been
having a history
of slow, continual
decline over the past
four centuries . One
after another, the world
has fallen to a succession of
indifferent overlords interested
in the world only for what limited
strategic value may still be extracted from
its dusty, sunbaked valleys and half-abandoned,
decaying cities .
What little industry remains is based around
lumber, mining, and small parts manufacturing .
Over a century of Clan occupation has resolved the
previous century’s massive unemployment issues
simply by compelling the surplus population to
relocate . The remaining inhabitants lead safe
but monotonous lives as the skeleton crew of
the planet’s critical infrastructure . A handful of
unique goods and sheer administrative inertia
are all that keep Wotan on the star charts, and
most of the planet’s youth dream of a future
anywhere but home . While rebellion is rife
across the Hinterlands, Wotaners no longer
possess enough of a shared identity to
rekindle resistance to the latest Clan masters .

If an opposition is not a player generated force, you can use the following tables to generate an appropriate opposing force .
There are separate tables based on general faction (Lyran Commonwealth, Clan Jade Falcon, Mercenary, Hell’s Horses, or Pirates) .
You then match the player(s) total Scale for their force to the tables below to generate the same Scale opposing force . If there are
two players on the same side, both with a Scale 1 force, you generate a Scale 2 opposing force to face them .
Roll 1D6 with a modifier for opposing force type . Each track will list a force type after Attacker
and Defender, listing Raider, Standard, or Garrison . In addition, the contract GM details may list the
opposing force as Militia, Regular, or Veteran . These are cumulative, so a Militia Raider force would
have a combined −2 modifier . If there are no force types, then assume a standard force with
regular experience (+0) .
Each force result will list a primary (’Mech) force, followed by a listing for support (non-
’Mech) and an alternate (’Mech) force . The primary force is always used . If Battlefield Support
Assets (from BattleTech: Mercenaries box set) or Total Warfare (for non-’Mech units) and
the BattleMech Manual (for Battlefield Support) is available, then use the support
force with the primary force . If those support options are not available, you can
use the alternate force with the primary force for an all ’Mech opposing force .
’Mech units will have their Gunnery and Piloting Skills listed in parentheses
after the unit (G/P) .
For Alpha Strike gameplay, use the Gunnery value of ’Mechs as
the Skill, and use a Skill of 4 for standard vehicles and infantry
and 3 for Veteran .
